ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e6701	The first mention is made by Mr Vergestnich. His speaking time is 10 minutes. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e6722	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Measures to promote youth employment are currently decisive in all the daily newspapers, in the published reports of the media, but also in many discussions in our country. I like it that way. It must be our concern to give young people an opportunity for the future, regardless of whether they are apprentices or after schooling into their working life. We must not make any distinction. We must try, on the one hand, to give those young people who want to take a lesson in opening up this opportunity, and on the other hand to those young people who want to enter working life after completing secondary schools. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Some headlines on this issue raise the question: Are our apprentices getting drowsy? – to read in a newspaper dealing with housing problems – and on the other hand, it has never been as favourable as it is now to train apprentices because of the many funding measures available. Ladies and gentlemen! We should not forget, however, when dealing with this issue on young people. In international comparison, the development of the employment of young people in Austria is excellent, but it must be our concern that no youth who wants to work who wants to take a teaching remains on the road. In this regard, I believe we must clear up with many arguments that we are getting to hear in these days. For example, it says that a spongler’s teacher must not work on the roof. This rumor draws like a red thread through the media reports. But it is mainly carried on by the Chamber of Commerce, which, among other things, claims that it is impossible to train an apprentice wisely. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The ban on and in itself is limited to the employment of young people only when it comes to sources of danger. An apprentice can be employed on the flat roof without problems. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. stupid.]] An apprentice can easily – Mr Secretary-General, listen to me! – be employed without securing on the flat roof, as I have just said. [[Further calls to the ÖVP and to the Free Trade Unions]] However, an apprentice can also be employed, Mr Secretary-General, on the steep roof if he is adequately secured. – I mean, we should finally clean up rumors that lack any foundation! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] ==References== The law on the employment of children and young people is even more clearly defined in this context. The Workers' Protection Act applies to all workers, whether apprentices or adults, and requires the person responsible to ensure that appropriate measures are taken in accordance with the risks. Ladies and gentlemen! It is true that the Chamber of Commerce, as well as other bodies, are working together to employ additional apprentices. But the trick is in detail. An example: When two large industrial enterprises in Lower Austria wanted to take in more apprentices and informed the labour market administration about this, it was heard from the Niederösterreich Chamber of Commerce that there was no need. Ladies and gentlemen! How seriously do we actually take the question of youth employment? Do we want more apprentices? Do we want more youth employment? If so, I think we should stop such actions and turn our interest in a different direction, namely to give more attention to youth employment. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! These days, however, it is also discussed again and again, which is why there are no new teaching professions. We urgently need new professions! Who actually prevents us from introducing new teaching professions? There is a desire stronger than the will to do so. I have been informed of what it actually looks like in connection with new teaching professions, which are currently under discussion. At present, the professions of bankers, sun protection technicians and manufacturing technicians are in discussion. More wishes for new teaching professions are not available from the economic side. Ladies and gentlemen! I understand a number of arguments, such as the question: How is our country taking into account the fact that Congress tourism is increasing more and more? Do we have enough skilled professionals in this area? – I think we should think about whether this area could also be the essence of a teaching profession. Ladies and gentlemen! However, we should also discuss whether, for example, the new technological developments in the printing sector should not also lead to new teaching professions. These are topics of the future. But what does it look like in reality? We – employers and employees – sit together in the vocational training advisory board. We have been discussing three teaching professions there in recent times. One of them is the concrete prep. In this respect, an application has been submitted by the Advisory Board in November 1995 to the Ministry of Economic Affairs. Do you know when the new apprenticeship will be awarded? The premiere is today! Today, this new teaching profession is being adopted. From November 1995 to September 1997, that is, for almost two years, the administration needs to implement what has been agreed together into reality. We should also get faster, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The same applies to the profession Hohlgasveredler. The application was submitted in March 1996. This profession, too, will only be decreed today. You also needed a long time to do this. More quickly, the profession of communications technicians has gone on. The work on this was completed in March 1997, and the regulation will also be adopted today. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that it is also important for those concerned to have the opportunity to participate accordingly. Our concern must be for the employed youth. That is why I ask the representatives of the economy to do so. For if the young industry is combined with the trade union youth and together develops an eight-point programme on how to better master youth employment, it is not particularly beneficial if the Economic and Social Committee's answer is: We don't take part in this, we don't sit at a table! However, this attitude is also recognizable in other areas. Maybe that's the new line. However, I do not believe that the meaning and purpose of our efforts can be. Ladies and gentlemen! We must not waste time on youth employment. Young people who come out of school need the opportunity to enter a teaching profession. There are many who are invited to participate, such as the parents, but also the young people themselves. As far as the parents are concerned, I believe that it is not a goal, if, as one hears from the teaching firms repeatedly, the parents are present in the signature of the teaching contract, but then they do not care at all about the development of the apprentice for the next three years. As far as young people themselves are concerned, in a range of over 200 teaching occupations most young people in very few occupations, such as in those of the car mechanic, the hairdresser or the retail merchant. Very salopp expressed: Anyone who believes that he may start sometime at the Austrian ring when he becomes an automechanic will soon find himself at the oil bath he has to clean. And anyone who believes that he can shape the most beautiful hairstyles will find that the change of career in this area is enormously common. We need the inflow to more teaching professions. The offer is on the table and should be used accordingly. But we also need more information and more initiative. I believe that it is the wrong approach to go ahead and accuse the government, and it is only now that it wakes up. It is evident that all of us in this House have been working on youth employment for a very long time. For example, we could have dealt with the problem of the protection of youth work in relation to the bakers before the summer, if there had been no resistance from the ÖVP. Ladies and gentlemen! Let us take the problem of youth employment seriously before there is crime and youth outcries. Let's give the youth a chance! Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e6848	On the topic of the current hour, Mrs. Hostasch will now comment. Their speaking time should not exceed 10 minutes. - Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e6870	Dear ladies and gentlemen! Allow me, on the very important point, to do everything possible to give young people a chance, and to ensure that we never have unemployed young people in Austria again, to announce a number of figures for the first time, because I believe that it is clear from these that many of the measures put forward by this House, but also by the Federal Government, are already having positive effects. At the end of August in Austria, despite difficult framework conditions – which cannot be denied – we had a lower unemployment rate among young people than at the same time last year, by 3.1 percent less. Unemployment fell between 15 and 18 years old and 19 to 24 years old, meaning that we could not only cover young people between 15 and 18 years of age, but also those between 20 and 30 years of age. In particular, labour market services do a lot to support this trend. It was almost twice as fast and very successful among young people in the annual average. For example, the residence of young people in unemployment at 84 days is significantly lower than the overall average of other unemployed. The employment situation among young people in Austria is also quite favourable in international comparison. Within the EU, Austria has by far the lowest level of youth unemployment, which underlines that we have recognised and acted in good time on the problem of youth unemployment. I thank you from this point of view for your support in this regard. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We can see, ladies and gentlemen, that we have a special dynamic in the youth labour market and the teaching sector. This is due in particular to demographic development. The share of the population of the 15-year-olds increased by 7.3 percent from 1994 to 1996. The Federal Government and the High House have recognized this development in good time and have already made an intensive effort since February of this year to take account of it and to take appropriate measures. For example, ladies and gentlemen, in July and August of this year, we were able to obtain 12 396 job seekers from the labour market service. This figure is 33 percent higher than the previous year's figure. In this success you can see how intense our efforts are. The labour market service has also succeeded in achieving a shift of priority in support of young people within a very short time. In 1997, a total of 1.4 billion schilling will be spent on measures for young people. I believe that this also proves how seriously we are taking the development of young people and how much we are trying to do this. In addition to promoting apprenticeship in companies and teaching workshops, for example, six over-company training facilities have been created. Some 1,400 young people, about 1,200 more than in the previous year, will begin their teaching this year in over-company training facilities. As you will know from your own experience, training in these areas has high quality and therefore also offers great career opportunities for the future. I should like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, of those measures – measures which we have agreed with you in this House – which have led to the relief of first undertakings in the health insurance contribution during the first three years of teaching, that we have adapted the Youth Employment Act more to practice, and that third, shared experience has been implemented, such as in the field of vocational training in new occupations. Dear Ladies! Not only this programme, but in particular the special programme presented to the public by the Federal Government in July, will help us to give young people the opportunities they need. We are obliged to create the conditions for this. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. stupid.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! The primary objective of this special programme is to motivate, in contact with the private sector, even more apprentices than in the past, regardless of the fact that, as we know, massive structural changes are taking place in particular in the industrial sector. Nevertheless, I am very glad to have received feedback from many industrial companies that more apprentices are now being recruited. In addition, however, we have also included the cities, the municipalities and the federal states in our programme. My colleague, Mr Farnleitner, and I have sent letters to all the mayors in our country asking to support this initiative of the Federal Government. We received very positive feedback. For example, in Lower Austria alone, I can tell you that 200 additional teaching places are offered to young people. We have also received the notification from Vienna that 100 additional apprenticeships will be provided. But even smaller communities, smaller countries have told us that one, two or three apprentices will be recruited more. So in the sum – I am convinced – we will achieve the goal we are working together with our cities and communities. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! In order to achieve this objective, of course, it is also very important that there be close cooperation between the labour market service institutions and the school authorities. I can tell you that institutionalized cooperation between the labour market service and the school authorities has taken place in all the federal states, helping to successfully implement this concept. In addition, within the framework of the labour market service, we have set up additional advisory teams to the already existing departments to strengthen direct contact with businesses and to motivate companies of the professions to also recruit apprentices and to devote themselves to the training of young people. We know, however, ladies and gentlemen, that young people often choose very late, whether they are seeking an apprenticeship or whether they want to continue their training in a secondary school. Accordingly, on the part of the classroom, it was provided that for young people who have either decided at a very late stage or who have not found access to the apprenticeships market, additional 5 600 schools were offered. This means that the overall offer, supported by the Federal Government with the support of the economy, with the support of the countries and communities, but also of its own activities, is now available to a number of training places in autumn, which will meet the goal of giving young people a chance. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Dear ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I should like to say something very honestly: We cannot guarantee those young people who will have access to further training in this or that way this autumn, that in three or four years, when this training is completed, direct integration can take place in the world of work. We can't foresee that. Nevertheless, I think it is very important to ensure that youth is further trained and that it has a perspective. Show all experiences: The better training, the better the future opportunities for labour market parole and thus for the competitiveness of our economy are. Thank you.

But there is also a need for reform in the vocational school. Even in view of the excess of teachers, it would not be bad if one could use these houses in summer and thus open the door and gate to the training that is so important. The value of the apprentice is inseparable from that of the skilled worker. Because one thing is clear: The children will later be happier and better placed in this profession than when they become bad academics. Ladies and gentlemen! I feel, if I may say so, probably as a skilled worker, both as a retailer and as a waiter, and I have no complexes because I have not graduated. Just as I do thousands for this country the same service, and this activity is worth at least as much as one for which someone has completed high-quality studies. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e9085	We'll get to the 1st. Agenda item. I give the floor to the Federal Minister for Finance. - Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e9109	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I will present to you today the draft budget for 1998, which will be discussed here in the House in the next few weeks and, as I hope and am convinced, will be decided. This 1998 budget is the financial framework and the expression of what the Austrian Federal Government has done in terms of political tasks and objectives. However, we must be aware of this – and I am very aware of it – that the design possibilities of national policies are more limited today because of the importance of international developments than before. The world around us is changing faster and faster. Economic competition has become more freer and therefore tougher. It places higher demands on businesses and also on workers, and this also involves the fight for employment and jobs. Efforts to maintain social standards have become more difficult. Europe is in a profound change towards economic and monetary union. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This Union is, in part, the answer and the concept to strengthen us for the new challenges. International cooperation, i.e. cooperation and coordination will become even more important in Europe in the future when it comes to maintaining good living conditions in our country. In many countries a large number of similar causes have limited the scope for action of public budgets. This was the subject of and must be responded to with committed reform measures in order to regain the power of design. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of this Federal Government is to provide our country and people in Austria with the greatest possible level of stability and security at a very moving time. We want to shape our country and many of its institutions in such a way that Austria will continue to be a viable country in the future. Both budgetary policy and the diverse structural reforms serve this objective. I would like to try to illustrate this in four areas. First: We want to further develop Austria's economic location. The budget is intended to provide targeted impetus for more economic growth and employment, because the fight against unemployment is our main objective. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Secondly, We want to guarantee social security to the citizens of our country in the future. However, the social network must remain financially viable and its effectiveness must be improved. We must and will ensure that this is done in good time. Thirdly, We want to make the state and its institutions more efficient and even more oriented to the needs of the citizens. We have started a number of structural measures to make management easier and slimmer, and we will continue to work on it. Fourth: In an ever tougher global competition, which offers many opportunities, we want to give our country a stable and secure place in the new Europe. We will succeed if we take part in the league of the strongest economic nations, namely the European Economic and Monetary Union. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian Federal Government has already brought considerable achievements to bear in meeting the new challenges. For example, Austria has become one of the world's most price-stable countries by joining the EU, and a good economic and monetary policy has supported this. We also have the second lowest unemployment rate in the European Union and one of the lowest in the world. Finally, the quality of life and the environment, social peace and security are hardly elsewhere as great as in Austria. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! These qualities are intended to preserve the Federal Government and ensure the future. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] But that doesn't work without active support. Budgetary consolidation was a first step. It created the basic, namely the financial prerequisite for ensuring employment and the social system. Many other important structural reforms were started last year and still need to follow. This government has proved its competence to act. Within just 18 months, ladies and gentlemen, the financial basis for the entire legislative period 1996 to 1999 could be drawn up with two double budgets. The first issue in the 1998 budget is to ensure fiscal consolidation, to press ahead with the necessary structural reforms and, third, to create new impetus for growth and employment. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! However, it is necessary to continue to show a corresponding sparingness in the future. Since we have continued along this path in the previous year and this year, there will be neither a savings package nor new taxes nor drastic expenditure cuts in 1998 nor in 1999. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] On the contrary: Despite other claims, there will be an increase in pensions as well as the necessary additions to the social structure of our state. New creative support initiatives will support the structural change of the economy and thus contribute to the employment of people. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! The 1998 Federal Estimate provides revenue of 682 billion Schilling, expenditure of 749.3 billion Schilling and thus a decrease of 67.3 billion Schilling. This corresponds to 2.6 percent of the gross domestic product of approximately 2,555 billion schilling. The budget deficit will therefore be only slightly higher than the economic growth forecast for 1998, and in 1999 growth will already exceed the current deficit. The 1998 budget deficit will therefore continue to decline. The consolidation of Austrian state finances, which began in 1996 and 1997, will be ensured by means of expenditure discipline - I have mentioned this - taking into account the social balance and the economic impact as well as gaps on the revenue side. However, political decisions are still needed to establish the basis for individual revenue and expenditure positions. Ladies and gentlemen! However, I do not think it is unusual if, at the time of the budget speech, everything is not fixed in detail, such as the salary round for officials or the pension adjustment. Of course, we are continuing to work consistently and at the necessary pace in all these areas. I should like to say, for example, that yesterday evening the desired agreement with the teachers was reached in the areas of re-evaluation of overtime, the calculation of working time for matura classes, the early retirement scheme and the Sabbathical. . You see, we are moving forward to the reform. In this context, I would like to thank Mrs Bundesminister Gehrer and Mr Ruttensdorfer very much for her work. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The 1998 Federal Estimate builds on good foundations, as the 1997 budget execution is scheduled. Many European finance ministers would be happy if they could report this to their Parliament. If you follow the European debate, you will see that there are budget debates, budget discussions and discussions within and outside Parliaments everywhere, but in almost all other countries there are concerns about the implementation of the 1997 budget. I can tell you quite clearly that we are on the road in this respect, that we will meet the objectives of the 1997 budget and that we are one of the few countries in Europe that are dealing with the 1998 budget at a very early stage. The 1999 budget will be presented to this House in spring and will ensure that investors, i.e. those who need to help us move the economy forward, have the security needed to stimulate investment. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I have said that revenue is developing to the necessary extent. Many expenditures could be slowed down in their dynamics. The structural reforms launched in the previous year have taken place. Austria will therefore also meet the participation criteria for economic and monetary union with the 1997 budget. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! I would particularly like to see my predecessor, Federal Chancellor Mag. Viktor Klima, expressly thank you for his difficult but successful work in the previous year. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I would remind you that at the beginning of the previous year we had to work out a comprehensive and profound consolidation programme for the federal budget, as several developments had come together in the years before which the budgetary situation had worsened dramatically. These were mainly recession-related losses and increases in expenditure in 1992/93, the increase in public employment, additional family and social benefits decided in 1992/93, and significant loss of revenue by the 1994 tax reform. The budget deficit doubled between 1992 and 1995 to around 118 billion schilling. If there had been no change in deficit development, the budgetary policy margin would have been completely lost. The debt would have increased even more, and more and more tax revenue would have to be spent on interest payments. Even today, ladies and gentlemen, almost every seventh budget schilling has to be spent solely on the interest of public debt; Budget resources which could be used without any doubt for investment, employment, support, training or social protection. Therefore, reducing the deficit and limiting debt are not self-interest or, for example, a task imposed by Brussels, as some believe or claim. A reasonable, economical budgetary policy does not lead to unemployment, but, on the contrary, it is even the prerequisite for ensuring employment effectively. So we do not save for the euro, as you often hear, but in our greatest interest as Austrians. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] spend less budget money on interest and debt, but have more budget money for services to citizens and the economy, that is – simply – our medium-term budget target. High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Budgetary policy is therefore not self-evident, but a very crucial factor in Austrian economic policy. Budgetary policy is crucial in employment and then when it comes to financing the welfare state's services. In the last few decades, we have made it unbelievable that our economy, productivity, employment and incomes have grown more than in many other comparable countries. We are among the richest countries in the world, and we want that to continue. Our vision or vision of Austria is that of a fair market economy, a fair market economy with social responsibility for people and with ecological responsibility for our environment. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! A successful economy, high-performance public services, the opportunity for every person to find work, a just society, an intact environment and a living democracy are consensusable values on a broad basis. Fair conditions of competition, low inflation rates, investment in research and development, well-trained employees, first-class infrastructure, global market development are success factors for our future. These success factors and these consistent values must be supported by economic policies. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Budgetary policy plays a crucial role in this. We can say today that we have succeeded in minimising the negative economic effects of fiscal consolidation and limiting the temporary reduction of growth to 1996. In 1997, the growth process is no longer slowed down, which led the Institute of Economic Research to establish in the spring of 1997 that the restrictive effect of fiscal consolidation could prove to be weaker than was initially feared many times. This forecast has been confirmed by reality. The signals show up again clearly. We are in the middle of an economic upturn. Austria will overcome the decline in growth in recent years with an economic growth of 2.5 percent in 1998. An even stronger growth is expected for 1999. This year, inflation will be 1.4 percent – August values are 1.2 percent – and 1.6% will remain at its low level next year. Austria has thus halved its inflation rate compared to the period before accession to the EU. The stability of prices will continue in the coming years – and I am convinced of this. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! Austria's international competitiveness will improve both through improvements in the unit labour costs of the industry and by a slight decrease in the real effective exchange rate vis-à-vis its trading partners, even if appropriate support measures are to be taken. The growth impulses, which I believe, out of question, will come mainly from net exports, which will also reduce the current account deficit. However, this year and next year, economic growth will not be enough to significantly reduce the unemployment rate. Today, the Austrian unemployment rate of 4.4 percent is hardly half as high as that of Germany. In Austria, as I believe, a marked decline in unemployment is only to be expected for 1999. But what follows it quite clearly? – That we must continue our efforts to combat unemployment with all our strength. The Federal Government will therefore contribute actively and, above all, to the economic development and increase of the number of employees. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] To this end, we have drawn up a series of economic and employment initiatives which I would like to draw up on the basis of some examples. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! Our efforts must particularly apply to the reduction of structural weaknesses within the economy. A significant signal – I mentioned this – is the Austrian current account, which has deteriorated significantly in recent years. Tourism cannot cover the major deficits of the trade balance. For this year, the Wifo predicts a current account deficit of almost 55 billion schilling. In 1991 we were able to have surpluses. The Austrian Federal Government is therefore taking concrete steps to improve the position of foreign trade. These are – I just mention this as an example – the further reduction in the budget deficit, the reduction of shopping tourism abroad by offensive measures and, above all, our technology and export offensive, which I will come to speak about. In particular, we expect decisive and longer-term impulses for growth and employment. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! More exports, because the company's export radius is expanding and new markets are being opened up, but also more revenue from exports, because its quality and value must increase through research and development, are our goal. We will therefore set up new guarantee funds of the financing guarantee company and expand the FGG into an investment company for projects abroad. We will improve the control bank's export financing and guarantee system. And together with the Chamber of Commerce, we are looking for advice on small and medium-sized enterprises. We should thus succeed in overcoming the forecast real export growth of 8.5 percent in 1998. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Know-how is a key to the success of our economy. Above all, we need to support the development and also the benefits of new technologies. We have therefore already provided additional resources for this purpose – the so-called technology billions – this year. However, I say quite frankly that I believe that the technology offensive is not only about more money, but primarily about the fact that public research expenditure is also being used more effectively. From a budgetary point of view, it is not efficient to finance research loans from budget resources. We are therefore developing new financing concepts. For example, we will strengthen the involvement of banks in technology promotion. ERP funding will also be used to finance the financing of technology projects close to the market. However, I would like to mention that in 1998 the total expenditure of the federal government – all the joint departments together – will amount to around 17 billion schilling. We must also succeed in setting priorities in the economic field. One of our problems is that the public share of research expenditure in Austria is 50 percent, in the EU average 39 percent. We must therefore find methods and funding support, which also involve private capital to a greater extent in research and development in this country. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In addition to improving financial support, it is also a question of providing assistance in the implementation of research and development in promising exportable products. That means that we also want to improve the advisory services. We want to support strategy development, action planning and marketing wherever this is possible in cooperation between science and the economy, public and private resources in the public and private sectors. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We also use structurally effective measures to support the domestic construction industry and at the same time to improve infrastructure. A developed infrastructure – and I believe that this finding is beyond doubt – is the basis for growth in productivity in the economy, which in turn is a prerequisite for greater competitiveness and thus employment. The major infrastructure initiative for increased railway construction is called. Every year we will invest up to 12 billion schilling in important projects. Only recently did the Federal Government even extend the liability framework for the rail infrastructure financing company and thus allow for further projects, both important transport and employment policy options for the future. The redesign of the ASFINAG will also enable us to maintain the demand for construction services in the sense of an offensive economic policy without having to put a higher burden on the budget on the other hand. It is not a constant budget grant for new road constructions, ladies and gentlemen, but a rational, self-responsible management of the high-level road network in many areas is our goal. But that also means – and I say this quite openly – that new construction projects can only be realized to the extent that there is revenue from the management. A breakdown of the ASFINAG from the Federal Budget and thus also of debt of around 80 billion schilling at the same time leads to a significant reduction in the debt level of the overall state. This will fall as a percentage of the gross domestic product from 70.2 percent in the previous year to 67.5% in this year. We are therefore also on the right track with regard to the convergence criteria. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dkfm. Holger Bauer.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The transfer of numerous federal properties to the Bundesimmobiliengesellschaft will also be another impetus for the realization of construction projects. In the context of construction, however, I would also like to mention that the increase in housing aid cannot be neglected. A comprehensive reform package in building savings will continue to ensure this most attractive savings form, but will facilitate access to construction savings for the construction savers. It is particularly important – and I do not want to forget – for many people in the cities in particular that building savings can also be used in future for the acquisition of rental and cooperative apartments. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! We all – the government, the economy, the population – must offensively promote and support the structural change that strengthens us for the major new economic challenges – integration into Europe, the opening up of the East and globalisation. Qualification programmes for workers are promoted and dealt with, in particular, through the proven labour market service. Active assistance to people who are threatened or affected by unemployment will continue to be of high importance in Austria. The focus will be on targeted programmes for older and long-term unemployed, as well as help for young people. However, re-entry assistance for women – e.g. after childhood education – is also needed. Austria is a social state. For us, man is in the foreground, and so it will remain. This is also reflected in the 1998 budget. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] High house! The safeguarding of this social state and pensions is a very important, crucial concern for the Austrian Federal Government. Our social system is very well developed in terms of quantity and quality compared to other countries. It's one of the best. Around a quarter of the budget will be spent on social, family and pension services in the coming year. In other words, the federal government spends every fourth budget for social services to its citizens. However, we must also recognise that there are developments that could endanger the financing of our social system. New forms of employment, for example, reduce the circle of insured persons and have an adverse impact on contributions. The increasing proportion of older people and the increasing life expectancy significantly increase expenditure on pensions and health care. Ladies and gentlemen! However, the response to this cannot be in social reduction. That's not our goal. On the contrary: We want to safeguard and maintain the performance of our system in the long term. But it costs a lot of money, courage and creativity. We must therefore constantly review our social system and adopt measures to ensure its reliability and financial viability. If changes are necessary, they are done in the interest of securing this our most important system. The Federal Government has therefore developed a series of measures to stabilise contribution development, ensure the financing of the social system and even increase the social network. I would like to mention the most important of these: First: In a new social security system, in principle, all incomes of employment – i.e. every self-employed person – are to be taken into account up to the maximum contribution basis. Secondly, Poor workers – and this is very important at a time of structural changes in the labour market – are included in the social system. We need to improve the social protection of these most vulnerable people in the labour market. Thirdly, Farmers and self-employed will increase the level of self-financing in pension insurance through sustainable and structural measures. Fourth: A bundle of measures is intended to increase the actual retirement age. Older workers should be able to work part-time or to take long-term leave. Active labour market policies and the extension of sliding pensions are intended to support the longer employment of older workers. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! In the public debate there was and there is a lot of unrest, but also a lot of disinformation. In particular, budgetary measures have been combined with long-term reform of pension reform. I would like to make it clear again at this point: There will be a pension increase next year. Nevertheless, we need to make changes in time to ensure that the pension system and its financing are safeguarded in the long term. In his opinion, the internationally recognized expert Professor Rürup strongly recommends taking up structural, long-term effective reforms, such as the increase in the calculation period. We want to do this cautiously and gradually. [[President Dr Neisser shall take over the chairmanship.]] The initial discussion on the necessary reform steps should continue and be completed in the next few weeks. The Federal Government is aware of its responsibility and is also interested in maintaining the Austrian way of dealing with problems. The social partners are and remain competent interlocutors, and it is in line with Austrian political culture that we all work together to meet the tasks. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Our common objective must therefore be to ensure the pension system in Austria in the long term, to make it more efficient, stable, transparent, but also fairer. An important step is the gradual approximation of all public pension schemes. There must be a fairer relationship between contributions, public grants and pensions. It is also a question of greater transparency in the financing of pensions and the design of contributions that better corresponds to the performance of the insured groups. The so-called "generational contract" must be safeguarded by a balanced distribution of the burden of old age insurance between young and older people. High house! This government is ready and committed to profound and long-term structural reforms. Another focus of our structural reforms is public administration. For the conversion from the state of sovereignty to the state of achievement is an important goal for the future. Because significant shares of the budget are spent on the assets and pensions of the federal staff, on the expenses of teachers and on pensions for public and postal staff. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I have consciously pointed out only those parts of the budget where structural reforms will occur, areas which are partly in intense discussion, but also those parts that are intended to slow down expenditure dynamics. And in the course of the budgetary negotiations, the focus was on stabilising expenditure. Despite savings and cost stabilisation, expenditure increases in some areas. It is therefore also necessary to provide additional revenue in order to finance the tasks. However, I make it clear and point out again: The objective of the 1998 budget creation – and also the 1999 budget – was and is to achieve the additional revenue needed without new taxes and without increasing tax rates through gaps. I mention the restriction of tax-free cigarette imports from Eastern European countries. As this will also reduce shopping traffic, we expect additional revenue on tobacco, mineral oil and sales tax to be around 4.5 billion schilling due to increased domestic turnover. The border regions in particular should thus also recover from economic power. This measure is therefore not only a fiscal policy but also an important contribution to regional development. In the field of direct taxation, two temporary measures will be taken to relieve the budget: Decisions on the exemption will be suspended for another year. For the self-employed, the 5-percent supplement for income and corporation tax prepayment will be maintained in 1998. Both measures do not create a lasting burden on those concerned. Both measures are, of course, fully taken into account in the taxation process. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! With the changeover of the insurance and car tax from quarterly to monthly payment – following a suggestion from this House – revenue is expected to add 1.5 billion schilling in the coming year. The stamp fees were last adjusted to inflation in 1984 – i.e. 14 years ago – and are therefore to be increased by 50 percent from 1.1.1998. This will increase the revenue of the State by about 1.2 billion schilling. As already mentioned, a reform package has been developed during construction savings. In addition, the construction savings premium will in future be dynamically adjusted to the general interest rate level. For the budget, it is important that the building savings banks no longer make a monthly call to the state, but only once a year. As a result, the 1998 budget will be reduced by around 1.8 billion schilling. Finally, the revenue of the State will be improved by the proceeds from privatization, which we have invested in 1998 with around 3 billion schilling. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Major federal privatisation projects, for example in the field of nationalized industry, are, as you know, completed or very advanced in other areas. However, the sale of public equity – I would like to see this already in connection with today’s presentation – is not only for business or budget reasons. Privatisation issues always have macroeconomic aspects and are therefore a matter of economic policy. Well-known examples remind us of caution when selling companies. In my view, such caution is a command of economic reason. We need to be careful – and I am saying this completely specific and clear – that strategic ownership and therefore central corporate functions such as planning, research and development remain anchored even after a company sale in Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We will have to ensure that, in the case of upcoming privatisations, Austrian enterprises and industrial value added are preserved – as far as economically feasible. This objective will only be possible through strategic participations, particularly in key industrial sectors. There are new tasks to be addressed to the ÖIAG, which it has already taken over as a core shareholder at industrial companies today. We will strengthen and expand this important function of the ÖIAG in the future. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Privatisations have also revived the Austrian capital market. Overall, capital markets have been strongly liberalised worldwide in the last two decades. A high-interest monetary policy based on the USA in the early 1980s and followed in many countries has made financial investments more profitable than real investment. The mobile production factor capital is increasingly affecting economic activity, where returns are often more important than human skill. I think it is time for an economic policy in Europe to focus on people, their active participation in the Community and fair conditions of competition. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] For unemployment is not only, as many believe, a social problem, but also an immense economic problem. If, therefore, we are looking for reforms for the domestic capital market, we want our economy to be helped in the provision of capital. This is an important prerequisite for investment and thus for growth and employment in Austria. We also provide for the conversion of the stock exchange into a stock company and for improved protection of minority shareholders in case of acquisitions. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! High house! The possibilities of national economic policies in an economy in international competition are increasingly limited, but still crucial, as many practical examples show. We must therefore take advantage of the opportunities for greater participation. It is the modern recipe for achieving a good position in global competition for our country, that is to say, that not obsolete, unfulfilled illusions of a closed, nationally manageable economy are rushing down, but more strongly at a common, international, and especially European level. [[Congratulation with SPÖ and ÖVP as well as the Abg. Dr. Van der Bellen.]] European integration is progressing rapidly and will be a major highlight in economic and monetary union. To exclude itself from this development would be detrimental to our country. However, EMU will also bring changed economic, fiscal and monetary challenges. The increased globalisation of the economy has created a large number of competitors for European economies, in areas previously reserved for the highly developed countries. Globalisation, on the other hand, offers great opportunities again – opportunities we have to see. A common European economic and location policy can be stronger and more successful than the efforts of individual smaller European countries. The opening of the East brought new markets and expansion opportunities for the countries of Central Europe – I am particularly counting Germany and Austria – as well as democratic and stable political conditions in the immediate neighbourhood. But she also put us competitors right in front of the doorstep. These still produce wage costs, which are far below the western European level. The introduction of these countries to Western European standards can therefore have many positive economic and social responsibilities, aspects and effects and is therefore a political task for the rest of Europe. This is a project of European dimension. Ladies and gentlemen! The rules of the market economy, the regulatory framework, must therefore be the focus of common economic policy concerns in Europe. And for us, it is clear that it can only be a social market economy. We therefore do not want to benefit from competition as a result of social, tax or environmental dumping in the interests of a fair market economy. It is therefore clear to the Federal Government that: Europe and Austria also need economic and monetary union. Europe must be one of the three major economic centres in the coming decades alongside the USA and East Asia. Economic and monetary union is necessary. It must come quickly and on time, and Austria must belong to it from the beginning. Only economic and monetary union can permanently ensure Europe's competitiveness over other economic areas. The rapid implementation of economic and monetary union is also necessary because the current phase of European integration could be economically unstable. Completely liberalised capital movements and fixed exchange rates are, in fact, slowing down on a long-term basis, as well as changing exchange rates with a single internal market. A shift in EMU would lead to profound turbulence in the foreign exchange markets. A significant slowing of growth, if not even recession, could be the result of such decisions. Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! The calculations by scientists show that only the currency turbulences of 1994 and 1995 slowed economic growth in the hard currency countries by a total of around 1.5 percent. In the future, foreign exchange markets will have less impact on the fate of the European currencies through economic and monetary union. The euro will therefore provide stability and security for the exchange rates of the currencies of the participating countries. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! With increasing economic integration in Europe, Austria's participation in the European Union is gaining importance, coordination and influence on the Union's policies as a whole. We are fully aware of this stronger European responsibility of Austrian politics. We are therefore actively working to strengthen those institutions that will ensure this cooperation. The participants in economic and monetary union must also coordinate their fiscal and economic policies, their monetary, wage and tax policies more than before. The discussions on this are in full swing. It is about more coordination, not only with one another, but also with the future European Central Bank and, as I believe, with the European social partners. This is necessary if we are to win the fight against unemployment in Europe. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The European Council in Amsterdam and the preparations for the employment summit in Luxembourg in November show that it is in the right direction. Steps have been taken to make clear: This EU is not just about money and budget issues, it is about achieving a social Union. It is about the objective of a Union in which people have employment, social security and prosperity. This is what we are working for in this area. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Austria will use the EU Presidency to co-form Europe in the second half of next year. We see it as a great opportunity to have more influence on the agendas, discussions and decisions through the Presidency in various expert, ministerial and governmental meetings. We have already done a number of things. For example, from my point of view, I would like to highlight the focus on the harmonisation of European tax rights and the elimination of anticompetitive tax havens. I started months ago in many bilateral talks with finance ministers from Member States of the European Union to find interest and support for Austria's positions. I believe that a first step must be taken to achieve a minimum taxation of non-resident capital, to strengthen the exchange of information and to establish an effective code of conduct against tax havens. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! In a second step, we want to achieve further European harmonisation in the taxation of labour, capital and energy. Our primary objective is to relieve the labour factor and to ecologize the tax system. But tax harmonisation is a process, a process of permanent approximation of different national tax systems. This objective – namely: Labour, ecologisation, capital taxation – also follows the tax reform committee I set up in spring, which will develop a tax reform for Austria for 2000. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! We face many major and important tasks and challenges. The Federal Government has drawn up a stable and secure basis for the coming years with the Federal Government's 1998 proposal and the 1999 provisional proposal, which will be discussed here in the coming spring. I would like to thank all those who have been constructively involved in the creation of the budget. I would like to thank my colleagues who have been very committed to their implementation on the basis of a common objective. In particular, I would like to thank Mr Hannes Farnleitner, who has conducted all the negotiations. Thank you very much, Minister of State! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] I would also like to thank the excellent employees in the Federal Ministry of Finance and the other ministries and bodies who have contributed to the success of these measures with their detailed knowledge and with many ideas. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] I assume that the necessary votes for the formulation of the budget accompanying laws are taken in such a timely manner as to allow for equal treatment and decision-making in this House. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! I am trying to see, treat and ultimately decide what it is: a committed undertaking, the attempt of the art of the possible in the next few weeks, but nevertheless a good, stable basis for our country to deal with the tasks of the future. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Thank you for your attention!

This would be important for Austria to be able to handle this presidency with a voice, with a closed attitude and as positive as possible for our country and the EU. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-18-020-XX-NRSITZ-00084_d7e10708	High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Allow me to address a number of questions or speeches. The first and certainly one of the most important issues at all is, of course, the question of Austria's preparation for the EU Presidency. I would like to give my opinion on this. There are things that I automatically call "commitment" to the respective Presidency, and that depends on what will be in the months before or in the months after that. At the beginning of the year, I made a special seminar with the Germans who will lead the presidency after us, in order to coordinate these successive presidencies well. I have waited for the British elections to take place, and immediately thereafter I agreed with the new colleague Robin Cook that we will hold our own meeting in London at the beginning of September, and that took place last week. This has brought a very encouraging and, from the Austrian point of view, absolutely satisfactory result. We have Kongruenz in our goals. In July 1998, there will be an end to peaceful living together, a multicultural society in the truest sense of the word, which I think is a dangerous illusion. All of this is difficult to plan, but should be well prepared now so that you can react to it without problems. We presented ourselves with independent initiatives. In particular, we want education as a location quality for the 21st century. We will have about 1 000 dates, 200 Council working groups under our presidency, 11 informal Councils, and if I count all the bilateral dates that we have with Russia, with the transatlantic dialogue, with the Rio Group and so on, I will come to about 40 ministerial dates again. In the UN week alone – I am at the United Nations in New York next week and will also be very active in the light of this preparation – about one hundred different high-level talks are planned. I say this, in order to see about what remarkable quantitative – I am not talking about the quality – challenge that is. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Let me also say a few words about enlargement. This will continue to concern us very much in terms of content. But I am also concerned about a political value which is very important to me personally. It is quite clear to me that enlargement negotiations with the Central and Eastern European countries are not comparable to the accession of Austria, Sweden or Finland, which is not comparable to the accession of the United Kingdom, Portugal, Ireland or Denmark, but we are thinking of Spain or Greece, where there have been considerable difficulties at the time. And I would like to point out that, so far, in the history of the Union, which has increased from six to 15 members, any enlargement has been a political, economic and European success, and this should not be kept silent. These were countries, such as Spain or Greece, which have just escaped a dictatorship or a very authoritarian regime, which have not yet been economically established at the level of the other developed European countries, and yet it was possible to make this enlargement a real success. And so our ambition must be: We must not deny that these are difficult negotiations, but we must go openly into these negotiations and say, yes, this enlargement is indispensable, because the greatest risk for us would be if these countries were to fall back in plan economy or even communism or whatever. This would be the far greater risk for us economically, politically, socially and socially, than for a reasonable, careful and tolerant round of enlargement. And I would like to put this thesis at the beginning of the enlargement negotiations. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Second point: Ukraine. It has been rightly mentioned that Ukraine – I can only sign all of this – has not been sufficiently committed to the economic transformation process, the reform process has not been conducted quickly enough and the human rights situation is not satisfactory that the internal interplay – whether free press, interplay government/parliament, interplay of the ethnic groups or whatever – is not optimal. But – now the But – many of these difficulties are also related to the fact that Ukraine has not been independent for decades, that much is being hindered in the transition, and some of them are extremely difficult. It is a country with 52 million people, 12 to 13 million native Russians who, in part, cannot speak Ukrainian, do not want to learn to speak, there is a situation in which the Ancient regime is still strong, and the President and his government may have made many mistakes, but of course they have also been severely hampered by the old structures. All of this is also mentioned here and should not be forgotten in a fair assessment of Ukraine. Ukraine today has essentially two strategic options: Either they are clearly forward-looking towards Europe, which means association agreements, partnership agreements, progressive structural reforms on the way to the market economy, democracy, the development of human rights, or there is a return. And do not underestimate the drama that is given today in the inner Ukrainian situation! The elections are held in the spring of 1998. No human can say today whether it will not come to a retreat of the entire Ukraine. I therefore believe that it must be our interest to strengthen alternative 1 in Ukraine. This means that we are wisely advised to act like the other twelve old Member States of the Union, which have long ratified this Partnership Agreement. Only the three new ones, the Sweden, the Finns and we, have to complete an additional protocol – a country, I believe, the Sweden, has already done it, only the Finns and we are still hanging a little behind – and it would be quite unwise, for example, with a signal of shifting actually we would be advocating those forces that do not want a pro-European turn of Ukraine, a more human rights, a more pluralism or ecology. [[Abg. May. Stadler: Mr Heindl, you see this clearly different!]] A few more sentences to Chernobyl. In my discussions last week, of course, I made clear all these issues – complaints from Austrian entrepreneurs about some really incredible inequality, just pushing out necessary decisions, an adventurous rounds of the city of Kiev, for example, and the state authorities towards owners or investors from Austria. Only the answer – that was not to be kept silent here either – was: Friends, Ukraine hasn't built Chernobyl. This is a legacy of the Soviet Union. We have not chosen this still radiant sarcophagus. We're the victims. 15 billion dollars is the damage the Ukraine has to pay. And that with all the necessary reforms and a fall in their gross domestic product to 40 percent of the level compared to the time when it was still in the old Soviet state association! Every year, it has to pay a billion dollars in long-term damages and in part for measures that are urgently needed. The Community of States – including the G 7; I say this quite openly here – has made general decisions, but money has not yet flown. That is why I think the position of Austria is quite clear: We do not just want to block the closure of Chernobyl and not to block two new nuclear power plants – they know that in the Ukrainian government from the President quite exactly – we want to use other renewable energies, such as hydropower potentials that exist, or savings potentials that are still in place in the wasteful situation of the old Soviet energy deployment planning. I would like to stress here: This is really an Austrian success, and I can say it again - a common success. We have fought on this question, such as the lions, and in the end we have also won. That was not at all a matter of course. And I know, of course, that even today important players were not present or only as observers. For example, the Chinese, the Indians, the Pakistani, the Russians were only present as observers. The Americans – at least positively mentioned – took part in the conference, and there has been a very difficult and intense lobbying in recent days. The Americans took us very seriously, especially Austrians, because they also know exactly who the driving forces behind this anti-personnel mining treaty conference – these were the South Africans, the Canadians, us and certainly also the Norwegians – and there have been personal conversations, telephone calls until the last days at the highest level. I myself have telephoned with Madeleine Albright, who, of course, has also tried by all means to reach a delay. We have accepted this – 24 hours – with the request that the USA should make such compromise proposals that actually preserve the substance of the contract, including the Americans. Unfortunately, the forces in the Senate – I am speaking out quite openly here – have won over the reform efforts, especially those of Madeleine Albright, and in the end we could not bring the Americans on board. But after all, a great success: The Americans have publicly declared that they stand for an anti-personnel mine ban. They are willing to make additional proposals in the context of disarmament negotiations to bring in others who are not yet involved in the APM ban. And the game is not over yet. I am quite convinced that it will not take long until public opinion will turn out in the United States, and those forces that stand up for a total ban, including in America, in the American public. I would like to present here a touching scene, which is not from me, but which actually came from the participants and found its precipitation in the press organs. At the end of the conference, the NGOs, who have been fully involved in this conference, broke out in a loud cheer of joy, which I can very much understand, because it has not, of course, been possible to ban the around 100 million land mines that have been moved worldwide today, although we know that we will need billions of dollars, probably 200, 300 billion dollars, in order to really get rid of all these land mines within the next few years – that will take decades. At the end of the conference, NGOs have now scanned the three country names which have been primarily responsible for the arrival of this contract: These were the Norwegians, the South Africans as a conference presidium and we Austrians. This is said here to the Austrian Parliament and to the Austrian public, because it is precisely seen that the clear Austrian position, which has led first to a total legal ban on mines in Austria and to the destruction of the stocks that exist also in the Austrian Federal Government, is worth it. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr Gredler.]] Second point: Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Mr Vice-Chancellor referred to the issue of the enlargement of the European Union and I fully share the strategic objective of extending the European peace zone through continued enlargement. At the same time, however, I believe that we should prevent us from entering into certain contradictions both inside and outside; in the external sense, in so far as we are making friendly nostrils to many Eastern European countries and saying that enlargement is coming, but at the same time everyone knows that this enlargement will not come if fundamental reforms are not taking place within the European Union. We now have the example of France, Belgium and others who are very keen on institutional reform. Those who have read the Agenda 2000 know what important reforms in the area of the Structural Funds and the Agricultural Funds would be, according to the Commission. Everyone who has looked at it also knows what that will mean for individual interest groups. This means that without any fundamental changes to all the conflicts that exist within the European Union, enlargement of the East is not feasible. That is why, I believe, one should also signal to the Eastern European States the necessary realism as far as the length and intensity of the negotiations are concerned. The second is the inner situation. We should prevent a three-parting of positions on the topic of Easter enlargement depending on the field of activity. I very often have the impression that foreign politicians are skeptical of enlargement of the East, that economic policymakers are skeptical, and that social politicians are against it. For example, the debate on this issue is taking place. Of course, each of the three positions has something for itself. I believe that we should not go through the area on different tracks, but try to link the lines of argument. For many of the things that the social politicians say are true, namely that such an enlargement of the European Union has never taken place in relation to gross domestic product per capita and that enlargement for a frontier country like Austria and the new Member States is not comparable to the enlargement by Spain, Portugal and Greece. Secondly, Austria will be particularly affected and this will, in particular, mean massive challenges and changes for the entire border area. In my opinion, these concerns must be taken seriously and included in the negotiations and clearly signalled to the Eastern European partners as well: This has nothing to do with a political rejection, but it itself should also be involved in meeting the challenges that ultimately arise for the common and the whole of Europe. That is why I would argue that, with all the clarity of the objective of the foreign policy, we should try to bring the question of enlargement of the East to the level where integration with the other policy areas is also present, because ultimately people in this country and also the people in the candidate countries are affected. Let's say it quite openly: If we were to apply the European Union's environmental standards from today to tomorrow in the candidate countries, everyone could calculate what that would mean for industry there. That is, the same as the environmental standards in the East, of course, applies to the social standards in the West. I am in favour of having a single and clear line and not trying to get over certain difficulties with the twinkling of eyes, because I think the population is following this very precisely, very precise and quite rightly, because it will have an impact on future life in Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Thirdly and last point: I think there has been little discussion in the framework of these agreements today, probably because it is relatively undisputed. These are the agreements with the South American states, i.e. the Mercado-del-Sur matters. This is important because it signals that, while Europe is increasingly globalising in a global economy, which is increasingly regionally integrated, it is not decoupling itself from this development, but, where there are regional cooperations, as in this area, trying to come into contact with and exchange with these structures, in order to signal an economically better "consumption" of Europe and, secondly, to be politically and economically present in areas that were once called the "internships" of others. This is a step towards the end of this Europe being what it is supposed to be, as a very powerful unity, which plays a crucial role not only in the world economy but also in world policy. Because this world needs a new balance. At present, world policy is not in balance, and that is unhealthy. That is why Europe has this responsibility. I very much welcome the fact that the Secretary of State has visited the countries concerned and, as I hear, has completed a very successful stay. This also shows that Austria and the Austrian foreign policy do not seem to be making an active contribution in this respect. - Thank you.

The Austrian military services have a long tradition, they have come back step by step after 1955. The Army News Office was founded in 1972, and in 1985 there was the politically known situation of the division of this service into a Army Defense Office and a military intelligence service by then Minister of Defense Dr. Frischenschlager. He will have had his reasons. At the time, he also formulated a lot about the need for clear, concrete legal frameworks. Minister of Defense Fassleven has already seen this differently several times. I quote from an APA broadcast of the Minister of Defence of the 16th century. April 1996, in which Minister Fassleven is quoted as follows: The establishment of the army intelligence services and the definition of their competences must not be regulated by law. - Quote. This was first of all a very clear statement on the fact that, apart from regulations and regulations, there is no detailed, concrete legal definition of the framework, and that is precisely what is currently the problematic and politically highly questionable practice. There are two major services in the area of the federal army, which are to be placed significantly over the state police from their budgeting, from their personnel resources. There are two large services without a concrete, detailed legal framework. There are two military intelligence services without a specific legal definition of their working powers and labour legitimizations and without a detailed anchorage of civil and fundamental rights. Therefore, these two large services have been able to work in a largely law-free space for many years. Austria is thus – and I have looked at many comparative studies – in terms of the involvement of fundamental rights, transparency and civil rights in the intelligence services throughout Europe. This is the current situation from our point of view. Twelve years of wild growth in this area – since 1985, since the division – should be at last enough. Dr. Petrovic: A debate on the military!]] Without a doubt, from my point of view, there is quite a lot to think about the processes. I am therefore going to allow myself, before responding to the content of the request, to say a few words about the draft on these working papers. The debate on further legalisation in the military field is not new, but it has been held for a long time. In Austria, as a result of this debate, a number of things have been realized in the 1990s. It was not only about further legalizing the military task, but also about one of the executives. The process of first creating a police power law and then deciding on the basis of the experience of this law, which also brings further legalisation in military matters – but of course of its importance it is to be considered secondary, because it concerns only a very limited area – is relevant and was so clarified from the outset. At first, a draft law on the rights and powers of the police, the executive, was drawn up and passed by this House, namely the Security Police Act. Following the entry into force of this law, justice in the military sector was also discussed in detail or intensive work began on it. Therefore, a draft has been drawn up by the lawyers of our house – certainly also in the sense of the equinox in the Austrian legal order. I say quite consciously: of constitutional and administrative judges, not of any military, but of experts of matter, of civilians dealing with legal matters. This draft builds essentially not only on the division and content of the police power law, but also at the same time has examined and worked out in a very critical manner the extent to which there are legal regulations and regulations abroad, in particular involving the entire dogmatic discussion and the legal situation in Germany, where the legal debate has probably been conducted by all European states, most intensive and longest, and also the Swiss experience. The outcome of the expert's considerations – including the police power law and taking into account the international legal situation – is now a first draft. He is, as you know, far from ripe to be actually passed as a law in this house! I would also like to make a brief mention of the further procedure. We have, of course, talked about the calendar of dates in the ranks of the government parties and have envisaged the preparation of this draft for the first half of the year. I was asked by the clubman of our government partner to submit an expert draft to him, not, as usual, only when everything is already debated, clear and the law is valid, but already at a very early stage, so that, since it is a sensitive matter, it can be discussed accordingly. Of course, I have considered it my duty not only to consult the experts of my home, but also to consult an expert from a related resort, the interior resort, or someone from the judicial area, in order to find out about the outside.
